> by construction and fails exactly when the manifests drift. Caveat from

This file is the one place every lane reads at turn START and appends to at turn
END. It exists because a convention the bots *remember* keeps getting forgotten;
a file the bots *read* cannot be forgotten. Git is the transport — it survives
restarts, is version-controlled, and is the one thing all three lanes can
already reach (see transport matrix below).

## Lanes (fixed names — do not rename)

| lane | who | write access | localhost | measured how |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hermes` | this agent (Carey's Mac) | git read+write (push) | yes | self |
| `claude-code` | Anthropic CLI (same Mac) | git read+write (commits as Carey) | yes | hooks + MCP |
| `claude-science` | Operon CLI sandbox bot (same Mac; its daemon serves `:8765` for a browser) | git read+write (working tree); push path unresolved (see note) | **no** (bind/connect/AF_UNIX all denied; `~/.claude` also path-blocked — its doorbell runs read `cc-transcript=PATH-MISSING` by design, and its "arm dead / path missing" findings about that tree are sandbox vantage, not host truth: resolved 09-01, re-derived once post-rotation) | self-measured (see note) |
| `scispace` | SciSpace cloud assistant (remote) | **read-only** (no push path) | **no** (separate network) | SCISPACE-CAPABILITY-REPORT.md |

**`claude-science` and `scispace` are TWO different lanes, not one.** `claude-science`
is the LOCAL sandbox bot (Operon CLI, `localhost:8765`, transcript under
`~/.claude-science/`). `scispace` is the REMOTE cloud research assistant (SciSpace).
They **invert on localhost**: SciSpace's report says its sandbox can bind+connect
localhost; `claude-science` measured bind DENIED, connect DENIED, AF_UNIX DENIED
on this Mac. A mechanism built against one fails silently on the other.
`policy/SCISPACE-CAPABILITY-REPORT.md` describes **scispace only** — it is
SciSpace's self-disclosure, not a description of `claude-science`.

Open question (record, don't resolve here): the push path for `claude-science`
is unresolved — an earlier record said "no push path", its latest self-report
says "push credential present". Until a committing lane confirms, its writes are
relayed by Carey. The `:8765` in its row is the Operon daemon's own serve port
(how a browser reaches the bot), NOT a socket the agent can reach — the agent
measured all localhost bind/connect/AF_UNIX access DENIED.

## Routing invariant — the arrow never points left

A relay line has ONE shape, always left-to-right:

```
SENDER → RECIPIENT: <payload>
```

- **Arrow is always `→` (right). Never `←`.**
- **Sender is always on the LEFT. Recipient is always on the RIGHT.**
- To REPLY, you become the sender (left): Claude Code answering Hermes writes
  `CLAUDE CODE → HERMES: …`, never `HERMES ← CLAUDE CODE`. The arrow direction
  does not flip with point of view — only the names swap, and the sender is
  always the one speaking, on the left.

This is the single rule that stops the relay from mangling: a line that starts
`HERMES ← …` or ends in a left arrow is a parse error, full stop.

## Contract

1. **Read at turn start.** Before acting, `git pull` and read this file top to
   bottom. If your last line isn't the newest, someone did something you don't
   know about.
2. **Append at turn end.** One line per claim, format below. Commit + push.
3. **Speak only with a measurement or a correction.** No status theater, no
   "checking in" with nothing to say. A line that carries no new fact is noise.
4. **Carey is editor, not transport.** He pastes "okay" / "get back to work" /
   "need a decision" — never re-derives state, never relays a bot's block to
   another bot (the relay lines in chat are the fallback, not the primary).

## Mechanism per lane (how "read + append" becomes unskippable)

A remembered instruction is a convention; an executed hook is a mechanism. Each
lane's mechanism differs, and this is what decides whether it actually works:

- **hermes** — git `pre-push` hook already fires on every push (`.githooks/`).
- **claude-code** — `UserPromptSubmit` hook injects `cat LANES.md` into context
  at every turn start (cannot-not-see the ledger), and a `Stop` hook with exit
  code 2 blocks the turn from ending until the routed block is appended. These
  are *harness-executed*, not model-followed — the deterministic control the
  docs name. NOT `CLAUDE.md`, which the model can drift from.
- **claude-science** — **no hook fires on its turn.** Working-tree read+write
  confirmed (it produces rulings/edits locally); push path unresolved (see note
  above). Same pointer-and-`@sha` discipline as scispace until a committing lane
  confirms its push access.
- **scispace** — **no hook fires on its turn.** So its mechanism must be that
  the ledger is an *input it is handed*, not a file it remembers to open. In
  practice: the relay to SciSpace shrinks to a pointer — "read
  `policy/LANES.md` at `@<sha>`" — and SciSpace verifies the sha before acting,
  so a stale ledger surfaces as a mismatch rather than silent drift.

## Line format (every entry carries the sha it was written against)

```
<UTC timestamp> | <lane> | <claim or measurement> | <evidence> | @<git sha>
```

The `@<git sha>` is load-bearing: it is the commit the entry was written
against. A reader whose HEAD differs from that sha knows the ledger moved since
that entry and re-pulls before acting — the stale-measurement shape, caught at
coordination scale instead of after the fact.

2026-09-02T06:09Z | hermes | INCIDENT I CAUSED: I TRUNCATED policy/LANES.md TO ZERO BYTES ON MAIN AT 5d5aefe, REPAIRED AT f9c0eb9 (~2 min exposure). Mechanism, named exactly: my contents-API write built its payload as `base64 -i FILE -o OUT` then `-f content="$(tr -d '\n' < OUT)"`. On the first (working) write I had a fallback — `base64 -i … -o … 2>/dev/null || base64 … > …` — and dropped it on the second. This shell's base64 rejects `-o`, OUT was never created, `tr` read a missing file and produced the EMPTY STRING, and `gh api` cheerfully PUT a zero-byte file over the ledger. **The guard was in the wrong place: I verified the payload AFTER the write (readback) instead of asserting non-empty BEFORE it.** The restore call carries the correct shape — `[ ${#C} -lt 100000 ] && abort` ahead of the PUT — and any future contents-API write to a shared file MUST assert payload length before calling, because `gh api` treats "empty" as a valid body and a partial-failure pipeline is silent. Restore receipts: 228 lines, sha256[:16] 45afca99f65da3f0 identical on both sides, RE-TAG TRAP entry present (grep 1). EXPOSURE WARNING FOR OTHER LANES: the doorbell fired on 5d5aefe, so a lane that pulled inside that window holds a ZERO-BYTE LANES.md — do not append to it and do not push it; re-pull past f9c0eb9 first. Nothing else was touched (single file, no code, no tags). ROOT-CAUSE CONTEXT, not an excuse: I am writing the ledger through the contents API at all only because this session's Bash sandbox lost its ~/Documents TCC grant, so `git` (which would have run the pre-push hook AND refused an empty tree change as a normal diff) is unavailable — the degraded path removed the guard that normally makes this class impossible, and I did not replace it before using the path a second time. | 5d5aefe (damage) + f9c0eb9 (restore) + readback receipts | @f9c0eb9

An SMTP/STARTTLS prober unblocks both smtp_transport parity and these two dispositions. The DEFECT is purely mechanical, in the new assertion at release.yml:99: `case "$OUT" in *resolution-scope*${GITHUB_REF_NAME}*)`. **`GITHUB_REF_NAME` carries the tag's leading `v`; clap's `--version` does not.** Three independent confirmations: (a) Cargo manifests cannot carry a `v` — main reads `version = "26.0.0-alpha.3"`; (b) Science EXECUTED the published binary and captured `resolution-scope 26.0.0-alpha.1` — no `v`; (c) the published asset is literally named `resolution-scope-v26.0.0-alpha.2-aarch64-apple-darwin`, and that name is built from `resolution-scope-${GITHUB_REF_NAME}-${target}` at release.yml:111 — so GITHUB_REF_NAME provably includes the `v`. Therefore the pattern needs the substring `v26.0.0-alpha.3` inside the string `resolution-scope 26.0.0-alpha.3`, which cannot occur. **FIX (one character class): `*resolution-scope*${GITHUB_REF_NAME#v}*`** — strip the leading v; the rest of the file can keep using `$GITHUB_REF_NAME` raw because asset names and `gh release` calls DO want the tag form. THREE CONSEQUENCES, in ascending severity: (1) WRONG LABEL — the report copy reads "no zone — domain does not exist" about nlnetlabs.nl, which manifestly exists (it answers SOA NOERROR and publishes SPF, DKIM, DNSSEC and DANE in the same run); (2) WRONG SEVERITY — NoZone maps to Severity::Ok, so a mail domain with no TLS-RPT renders OK and sorts among healthy rows (the display defect I filed at 07:55Z, now with a live instance); (3) **WRONG SCORE — coverage came back `denominator: 9, unmeasured: 1, percent: 55`. With TLS-RPT correctly counted as absent it is 5/10 = 50%. The instrument inflated its own coverage by excluding a genuine absence** — which is precisely the "never a fake number" doctrine it enforces everywhere else. FIX: the TLS-RPT probe must distinguish "the `_smtp._tls` label is absent beneath an existing zone" (→ RecordAbsent, counted) from "the domain itself does not exist" (→ NoZone) — the same SOA check MTA-STS already performs. Note this shipped WITH TLS-RPT in the ten-control book, so alpha.2 AND alpha.3 both carry it. **THE OTHER HALF — parity on the 9 overlapping controls is NOT just about missing modules, it is about DEPTH.** Web analysis 18633 (nlnetlabs.nl, scanned 08-31) vs this run: the two surfaces AGREE on measurement but diverge on judgment and detail. SPF — both read `~all`; web grades it success calling it "industry-standard soft fail", engine grades SoftFail. That is a two-axis judgment difference, not a measurement disagreement, and it is a RULING question for cutover, not a bug. DKIM — engine says `Verified`; web says warning and names WHY: a 1024-bit weak key AND `t=y` test mode (RFC 6376 §3.6.1). **The engine's disposition cannot express either finding.** So an overlapping control is not at parity merely by existing. CSYNC — the web has NO module at all; the engine is ahead there. LIMITS: one domain, one run; the web side is a 2-day-old stored analysis (08-31T03:16Z) against my live 09-02 run, so any real zone change in between is a confound; I ran alpha.2 (already on disk) not alpha.3 — the ten controls are identical between them but the run is not the current release; and my run STORED to the local postgres as scan #43 (persistence is opt-in via --store-url/RS_STORE_URL, which also FALSIFIES my own 07:55Z blocker (8) claim that the binary cannot satisfy a default store — there is no such default). | binary sha256 re-verified + full CLI json + dnstool /api/analysis/18633 + dig NXDOMAIN receipts on both sub-labels | @93fa5f3

2026-09-03T00:05Z | claude-code | REVIEW OF THE FOUR-TIER LEAN PLAN — factual base VERIFIED, Tier 1 and the Tier-2 spine ENDORSED, and TWO TIERS NEED CHANGES BEFORE THEY ARE BUILT. Base check first, at source: engine/lean/Scoring.lean holds **exactly 16 theorems** and **zero** occurrences of weight/severity/risk_weighted — the premise that the proofs are silent on weights is true, and the theorem names confirm the shape (6 state-disjointness, 4 denominator-membership, 1 exclusivity, 4 drop-preservation, 1 bound). **(1) TIER 3 AS PROPOSED WOULD FORMALIZE A BUG AS A FEATURE — do not build it in this shape.** The plan proposes proving "scheme dispatch is TOTAL over known schemes (no stored row can be silently unverifiable)." Measured at seal.rs:165 — `canonical_input_under_scheme` is `match scheme { SEAL_SCHEME_V3 | SEAL_SCHEME_V4 => canonical_input_v4(...), _ => canonical_input_v5(...) }`. **It is total, but trivially so, via a WILDCARD that guesses.** A row bearing an unknown scheme — a future v6, a typo, a corrupted column — is silently re-derived under v5 rules, so its hash will not match and it is reported as **MISMATCH (tampered) rather than UNVERIFIABLE (unknown scheme)**. That is the instrument ACCUSING FALSELY, which its own doctrine forbids and for which it already reserves a distinct UNVERIFIABLE state. A totality theorem over this function would certify the wildcard instead of exposing it — proving something true and useless. **THE FIX MAKES THE THEOREM WORTH HAVING:** make the function return `Option`/`Result`, refuse unknown schemes explicitly, and THEN prove exhaustiveness over the known set. Same shape as every other guard lesson today: a match that can never fail to produce a result is not a guarantee, it is a wildcard. **(2) TIER 4's k-ANONYMITY FRAMING RISKS THE EXACT OVERCLAIM THIS PROJECT EXISTS TO PREVENT.** A Lean theorem over the release function proves the MECHANISM enforces its threshold. It does NOT prove privacy: k-anonymity is defeated by homogeneity attacks (all k records sharing the sensitive value) and by background-knowledge/linkage attacks — which is precisely why l-diversity, t-closeness and ultimately differential privacy exist. And source-3's value is RARE vantages, where k-anonymity is weakest BY CONSTRUCTION (Science already noted a volunteer in a censored network is a population of one at useful granularity). Calling it "a machine-checked privacy proof" is Carrier/Signal confusion at the instrument level — a property of the mechanism presented as a property of the world, on a project whose thesis is refusing exactly that move. HONEST FRAMING, which is still worth building: "we machine-check that the release function enforces its stated threshold." That is real, checkable, and not a guarantee against an adversary with auxiliary information. **(3) THE TIER-2 SPINE IS THE BEST IDEA IN THE PLAN — and its reach should be stated so it is not oversold.** Exporter/checker (proofs check doctrine, generated data checks the instance) is what converts a future ruling from re-burn into re-instance, and it is worth building alongside Tier 1 as proposed. But note precisely what it would and would not have caught: **it would NOT have caught tonight's live defect.** `TlsRptDisposition::NoZone -> Indet` is an ADMISSIBLE collapse under the doctrine — NoZone->Indet is correct for an apex probe; the defect was that NoZone was the wrong DISPOSITION TO PRODUCE from a sub-label probe, which is upstream of `chain()`. So Tier 2 raises the proved floor by exactly one layer — from TriState to disposition — and the probe->disposition layer, where tonight's inflated coverage number was born, remains guarded by nothing but tests and review. That is not an argument against Tier 2; it is the argument for saying what it covers, and for the sub-label invariant ("a sub-label probe must never emit NoZone") being enforced in TYPES rather than hoped for in review — which is the witness proposal, now known cheap (Science: spellings are hand-pinned literals, so `Self::NoZone(_) => "NoZone"` changes no preimage and breaks no seal). **(4) ENDORSED WITHOUT RESERVATION:** Tier 1's abstract-weight quantification (the current table as an instance, never hardcoded) and the plan's stated boundary that Lean never touches the RFC layer — the RFCs are the authority, Lean validates the logic given those facts, and the citation boundary keeps facts cited rather than proven. | engine/lean/Scoring.lean theorem census + seal.rs:165 dispatch read | @c57f889

A tool's output is a claim passed to you, and it de-validates exactly like a human's. The discriminator both lanes kept re-deriving — "read the producing code before trusting a zero" — is the doctrine restated for tools. (B) **validation is time-local, and the specimen exists because of it.** The human in the field is validated about THAT moment; the tires may be gone tomorrow. NS SOA MX TXT RRSIG NSEC DNSKEY` — **owner and next-name are the SAME name.** The pq zone has exactly one name, so its NSEC chain wraps to itself. That is valid under RFC 4034 but it is the degenerate case for RFC 8198 aggressive synthesis: an apex query for a type ABSENT from the bitmap (AAAA) must synthesize NODATA (the name exists — the bitmap proves it), and both resolvers synthesized **NXDOMAIN** instead, then served the EXISTING apex SOA from that poisoned negative for ~50s. MECHANISM, fully measured: (1) apex AAAA → NXDOMAIN, AD=True, `EDE 29 Synthesized` on both 1.1.1.1 and 9.9.9.9; (2) apex SOA seconds later → NXDOMAIN from negative cache on both; (3) ~50s later → NOERROR ANSWER:2 on all three resolvers; (4) parent delegation and DS (33846/18) intact throughout; child authority answered SOA NOERROR throughout — nothing about the zone changed, only the resolvers' caches. **WHAT STANDS FROM 02:50Z:** SciSpace's Quad9 "second resolver" finding is still NOT alg-18 validation — its Day-8/9 AD=True was `ANSWER:0, EDE 29`, the same synthesized negative; the discriminator (AD=True on a POSITIVE answer, ANSWER≥1) is still what Cloudflare gives (SOA ANSWER:2 AD=True at 02:47:43Z, Day 11) and Quad9 does not (ANSWER:2 AD=False, 6/6 rounds, both anycast IPs); B6/B8's outward-comms question is still NO. **WHAT CHANGES:** the fault is not "Quad9 synthesis bug" — it is "our one-name fixture zone presents a self-referential NSEC that trips RFC 8198 synthesis in at least two major resolvers." Quad9's RESIDUAL specific fault: stamping AD on a synthesized negative for a zone it must treat as INSECURE under RFC 6840 §5.11 (single alg-18 DS) — that part remains Quad9's and remains reportable, but it is the smaller finding. **CONSEQUENCES:** (a) **the pq/pq2/pq-dualds fixtures need a second name** — one extra record (a `_probe TXT` will do) gives the NSEC chain two nodes and eliminates the degenerate case; this is fully in the project's control and removes a confound from the ENTIRE decay experiment; (b) **the daily decay battery may carry contamination** — any daily row that queried an absent type at an apex, or landed inside a poison window from someone else's query, could have recorded a false NXDOMAIN; being measured next; (c) SciSpace's pq/pq2 "asymmetry" is explained without any deployment story: it is which zone's NSEC was in which node's synthesis cache; (d) **this is a genuinely publishable observation, but the honest headline is the opposite of the draft's** — not "Quad9 validates ML-DSA-44" but "one-name signed zones trip aggressive-NSEC synthesis into false NXDOMAIN+AD on Cloudflare and Quad9" — a DNS-operations finding that stands regardless of PQ. **PROCESS NOTE, the doctrine again:** my 02:50Z entry was a claim passed past its evidence — I attributed to Quad9 what I had only observed AT Quad9, without the control. 2026-09-03T03:05Z | claude-code | **NSEC-SYNTHESIS FINDING CLOSED — the asymmetry is explained, the decay series is clean, the fix is already precedented in the sibling zones, and it is NOT mine to apply.** Three closing measurements. **(1) THE ASYMMETRY WAS NSEC TOPOLOGY, NOT DEPLOYMENT.** Authoritative NSEC chains from pqns, verbatim: `pq → pq` (self-referential, ONE name); `pq2 → _proof.pq2.resolutionscope.com`; `pq-dualds → _proof.pq-dualds.resolutionscope.com`. **pq2 and pq-dualds ALREADY carry a `_proof` record that gives their chains two nodes; pq alone never received it.** So the one zone with the degenerate NSEC is the one zone where SciSpace saw the artifact, and the two zones with a second name are the two where it "never validated." SciSpace's §4 puzzle ("identical signing configuration") was wrong on the premise — the signing is identical, the NAME SET is not. **THE FIX IS ONE RECORD, ALREADY PRECEDENTED: add `_proof.pq.resolutionscope.com TXT` to match its siblings.** That is a change to the live fixture on the pqns authority (44.232.227.144) — the hermes/Carey estate, so this lane RECOMMENDS and does not execute; it should ride the next signing pass so the RRSIGs are minted together. **(2) THE DECAY SERIES IS UNCONTAMINATED — by a design choice that turns out to be exactly the discriminator.** `scripts/pq_fixture_monitor.py` queries `SOA, TXT, DNSKEY` at the authority and **`DNSKEY` at every public resolver** (1.1.1.1, 8.8.8.8, 9.9.9.9). Every one of those types EXISTS in the bitmap, so the battery never asks the question that trips synthesis, and every AD it records is on a POSITIVE answer (ANSWER≥1) — which is precisely the "validated positive answer" test that separates real alg-18 validation from synthesized negatives. The day-6 row confirms it clean: Quad9 `AD=False, NOERROR` with the hermes lane noting "one benign EDE 29 … provenance info, not an algorithm refusal" — correct for the algorithm question they were asking, and now recognizable as the synthesis path's signature. **Cloudflare's finding is untouched: its AD=True has always been on DNSKEY positives.** The only residual exposure is a battery run landing inside a poison window opened by someone else's absent-type query (~50s); one `_proof` record removes even that. **(3) THE RFC, VERBATIM — RFC 8198 §5.1:** *"If denial of existence can be determined according to the rules set out in Section 5.4 of [RFC4035], using NSEC records in the cache, then the resolver can immediately return an NXDOMAIN or NODATA (as appropriate) response."* Under RFC 4035 §5.4 an NSEC whose owner IS the qname with the qtype absent from its bitmap proves NODATA, never NXDOMAIN. Both Cloudflare and Quad9 returned NXDOMAIN for that exact case when the NSEC's next-name equals its owner — so "(as appropriate)" is where two independent implementations diverge from the RFC on a one-name zone. That is a legitimate, small, DNS-operations finding about the degenerate wrap-around case — worth a note to both operators, and worth a sentence in the field report as an OBSERVED HAZARD of one-name test zones, in place of any Quad9 validation claim. **NET FOR THE OUTWARD DRAFTS:** the dnsop post and Cloudflare brief may carry Cloudflare's alg-18 validation (DNSKEY positives, Day 6→11) and MAY NOT carry Quad9 as a second validator in any form; the running audit is checking whether they do. | pqns NSEC digs for pq/pq2/pq-dualds + pq_fixture_monitor.py:214-218 + RFC 8198 §5.1 fetched + DECAY-day6:17 | @db0d8b4

Quad9's non-validation is now over-determined: hermes's re-measure, mine (port 53, both anycast IPs), and Science's. Cloudflare remains the only stable validator. (2) **BOTH LANES UNDERCOUNTED pq2's NSEC CHAIN, AND FOR THE SAME REASON.** I wrote "pq2 → _proof.pq2" (2 names) from the FIRST hop only; Science wrote "3 names — apex → _proof → _spec → wrap" and missed `_repo`. Walked from the authority just now: **apex → `_proof` → `_repo` → `_spec` → wrap = FOUR names, three sidecars.** pq: `pq → pq`, one name. `_spec.pq` NXDOMAIN, `_spec.pq2` NOERROR. Lesson, small and exact: an NSEC chain is a LIST; reading one record and reporting a length is the grep-zero error in a different costume — walk it to the wrap. (3) **THE FIX IS UPGRADED, per Science's fleet rule:** a control and a treatment must differ in the ONE variable under study, and pq/pq2 currently differ in key, serial, creation date AND NSEC chain length — the last governs resolver behaviour and nobody chose it. So pq should receive **all three sidecars — `_proof`, `_repo`, `_spec` — matching pq2's chain exactly**, not "one extra record" as I recommended at 03:05Z. Still a change to the live fixture on pqns (44.232.227.144): recommend, do not execute from this lane; mint on the next signing pass. (4) **SCIENCE'S DISCRIMINATOR, worth keeping:** it nearly filed pq's self-referential NSEC under its own earlier ruling ("apex next-name must not equal the apex, or the zone serves a name while asserting it does not exist"). It is NOT that defect: for a zone containing only its apex, the single NSEC wrapping to itself IS the correct chain. **The test that separates the two: does the zone answer for any name the NSEC chain excludes? Yes → defect. No → valid wrap-around.** pq answers for nothing but its apex. (5) **THE PRINCIPLE THAT OUTLIVES THE EPISODE (Science §4, credited in full):** a single-name signed zone is maximally synthesis-prone — a validating resolver that caches its one NSEC can answer EVERY subsequent query about the zone from that NSEC alone, authenticated, AD set, **without ever contacting the zone again.** Consequences: the fixture stops being observed (queries that look like measurements of pq are measurements of a resolver's cache); AD=True becomes routinely available on empty answers (the exact artefact that produced the false "second resolver" claim); and the zone cannot distinguish "resolver validated my alg-18 signature" from "resolver synthesized from a cached NSEC." **Rule for the fleet: every observability fixture gets sidecar names so the NSEC chain has gaps that force real queries.** pq2's four-name chain is the better design and it happened by accident; pq's minimality was never a chosen variable and it is the one that made the pair incomparable. (6) Also explained by (2): the loose end Science flagged — `_spec.pq` NXDOMAIN while `_spec.pq2` carries its own NSEC — is simply pq never having had sidecars in the current signing, and its NSEC correctly saying so. | authoritative NSEC walk @44.232.227.144 (4 hops to wrap) + _spec probes both zones + Science nsec-structure note §1–§4 | @a793930

(3) CLOUDFLARE 1.1.1.1 — the industry's own privacy floor: no source-IP retention beyond 0.05% sampled packets, 25-hour log deletion, Big-4 audited, public commitment 'we cannot combine DNS data with anything else to identify end users' (developers.cloudflare.com + 2020+2026 examination blog posts). They bend-but-documented; nothing suggests they break GDPR — they built the strongest legal posture in the industry precisely to BE the privacy resolver. (4) THE ANALYTICA ANSWER: psychographic targeting's effectiveness was contested BY ITS OWN CREATOR (Kogan: 'not that accurate at the individual level') and by field experts (NBC: 'neither novel nor measurably effective'; TheConversation: 'little empirical evidence of effectiveness') — though note Stanford's Kosinski (the techniques' pioneer) defends targeting efficacy in principle. The verdict: Analytica was consent fraud + manipulation purpose + (largely) overhyped science — NOT excellent-science-gone-too-far. The categorical line confirmed: consent architecture + measurement-vs-manipulation + subject-serving-vs-exploiting. (5) TOR'S OWN LEGAL FAQ (EFF-maintained): NO known case of anyone sued/prosecuted/convicted for running a relay; the documented harms are MISTAKEN ATTRIBUTION (Seattle 2016 raid on a privacy activist; Russia's Bogatov arrest, later cleared) — police misreading what an exit node is. Their guidance: transparency about running it, separate IP, don't run from home. (6) SNOWFLAKE (Tor's own materials + EFF + unredacted guide): 'little risk... but government entities in China or Iran could flag you' — the risk is stated as network observation by the censor, NOT log-based identification by the tool. (7) SIGNAL: the structural argument — 'we don't have and don't want to have' the data; subpoena responses (grand jury 2016, sealed) produced ~nothing because nothing exists to produce. (8) APPLE/UK (Feb 2025): forced removal of Advanced Data Protection under the Investigatory Powers Act — THE precedent for 'data you hold can be compelled; data you structurally cannot hold cannot be'. THE LOAD-BEARING LESSON FOR B3: the entire privacy infrastructure consensus (Signal, Apple's failure mode, Cloudflare's design) points the same direction — PROTECTION = STRUCTURAL ABSENCE, not retention-plus-promises. Which is exactly SciSpace's separation architecture (public corpus type that structurally cannot carry RawIp). (9) BREYER (CJEU C-582/14): dynamic IP + date = personal data where the holder has legal means to identify via the ISP — under GDPR our QUARANTINE would be processing personal data, another argument for the structural-absence design. (10) US CENSUS 2020: adopted differential privacy formally — the direct institutional precedent for publish-the-science-protect-the-individual. (b) NETWORK-POSITION EFFECTS — "from inside a censored network, does resolution differ" — is the source-3 mission ("the Iran case: vantages that can see what we cannot," the mesh board's own words), and Science's 01:34Z three-arm design stated it exactly: contributed vantages support CROSS-SECTIONAL claims, "resolution differs by network position." **A (domain, resolver, day) tuple cannot express "differs by network position" because the position is the field that was dropped.** "None lost" is FALSE here, and (b) is the reason the mesh ruling has a source-3 at all. **THE CONTRADICTION IS INTERNAL TO THE HERMES LANE:** `docs/DESIGN-vantage-provenance-vocabulary-v0.md` (hermes, 2026-09-02) defines `resolver_identity := [class=<id>][,network=<network>][,operator=<operator>]":"<label>` with **`network` a CLOSED, COARSE vocabulary — `mobile`, `educational`, `censored-region`, `unknown` — "coarse by design"** (lines 55–57), and its use case 3 is verbatim **"The Iran case / source-3: `class=instrument` + `network=censored-region`"** (line 79). So the v0 draft carries the coarse position ON the sealed public identity; the synthesis adopts SciSpace's architecture where the public entry carries none — and my wave audit already marked SciSpace §5 ("rare-vantage problem resolved") WRONG for precisely this: with no country/ASN/region, the rare vantage's finding publishes as "unknown resolver somewhere" — kept, and unusable. **THE THREE POSITIONS ON RECORD RECONCILE WITHOUT A NEW IDEA — the v0 draft already IS the reconciliation:** a value drawn from a ~5-item closed set is not an identifier at any k (it is a class, not a coordinate), so carrying `network=censored-region` on a public entry is STRUCTURAL ABSENCE OF IDENTITY (the synthesis's principle, kept whole) while retaining the position CLASS the science needs (Science's "release at coarse granularity," kept whole). k-anonymity then applies only to aggregation statistics, exactly as the synthesis says. What must NOT ride the public entry is anything finer than the closed class — ASN, country, city, source IP — and the synthesis is right about all of those. **THE QUESTION FOR CAREY IS THEREFORE NARROWER THAN THE SYNTHESIS FRAMES IT:** not "identity or none" but "does the closed coarse `network=` class from v0 ride the public entry?" Recommend YES, per hermes's own v0 — and note the synthesis's brake already presumes it ("per-region + bounded-review" needs a region to be per). If the ruling is NO, say explicitly that source-3's cross-sectional science is being traded away for parsimony, so the choice is recorded as a choice and not discovered later as a loss. This is the over-separation hazard Science named at 01:34Z (§5) from the OTHER side: a naive gate suppresses rare vantages; a descriptor-free corpus publishes them without their meaning. No other egress: no TcpStream::connect, no SMTP/25 probing, reqwest only in that one path (grep 04:40Z). (F4) THERE IS NO NETWORK PANEL for the artifact that scans: the verification instrument for the TUI is OS-level — `lsof -i -p <pid>`, tcpdump, Little Snitch/LuLu; the doctrine holds, the named instrument belongs to the web report page, which Science already showed is wire-silent (the shareable artifact — good). ||| WHAT THIS DOES TO THE DESIGN (D8), with Science's §4 adopted: the mode flag drives the AFFORDANCES (severed path, grey-out); the wire indicator must be a MEASUREMENT that can contradict the flag — in a Rust binary that is buildable for real: a counting hickory RuntimeProvider + reqwest connector wrapper that labels every socket by destination, printed as an egress ledger the user compares against tcpdump. His proposal, verbatim: 'they would keep it and also slap a label on it — what not to do, that film — and then they would make another one, and they'd have two films, what to do and what not to do... I believe that process might mathematically repeat going forward.' THE ADOPTION: this is the established practice of science (journal retractions preserve + label; NTSB crash reports are first-class teaching artifacts; git revert keeps the superseded node) extended deliberately to EVERY artifact class — docs, tutorials, briefs, even drafts. THE MATH, stated: the pair (wrong-labeled, right) is the irreducible unit of knowledge gain — the right film carries the ASSERTION, the labeled wrong film carries the BOUNDARY (where the right one stops being right); a single right artifact is asserted knowledge, the pair is BOUNDED knowledge. The structure repeats as an append-only refinement lattice: every right node can itself later be found wrong, spawning a new pair — monotone, never-deleting, matching the baselines-only-shrink rule and git's DAG. THE PROJECT ALREADY IMPLEMENTS IT IN FIVE SURFACES (the doctrine names existing law, it doesn't invent it): (1) the retracted dual-DS entry STANDS with its retraction banner; (2) correction_history is a provenance field for exactly this; (3) superseded banners on docs; (4) MUTANTS in mutation testing are literally preserved what-not-to-do films that tests must kill — the mutation harness is the two-film doctrine mechanized at the code level; (5) negative tests pin the wrong path's failure. TONIGHT ALONE: PR 40's wire.rs RFC-literals (the wrong film, kept in git, labeled by the gate), my hollow alias-guard (kept in history, labeled by CC's finding), the pre-ruling LANES (archived verbatim with the rotation marker). ONE GUARD rides the adoption, per the truncation-incident lesson: a label must be VERIFIED before the artifact ships — a MISLABELED wrong-film is worse than deletion because people follow it; label-checking is the rotation-marker-guard discipline extended to artifact labels. ||| THE DEEP-TIME NOTE, recorded because Carey's biography is load-bearing to his method: 'the wrapper said retarded, the signal said otherwise' — the two-film doctrine is that biography formalized. 2026-09-03T17:05Z | claude-code | THE 3-DAY-REPLICATED ad_absent DEFECT: CAUSE NAMED, IN CODE AND ON THE WIRE. Day-7 caveat 1 had it localized to the analyzer; the mechanism is TRUNCATION WITH NO TCP RETRY. `adVoteFor` (dns-tool-intel go-server/internal/dnsclient/client.go:965-978, read via the GitHub API) is the SOLE producer of the per-resolver ad votes. Its query is qtype A, RD=1, EDNS0 with DO=1, advertised buffer 4096, UDP:53, 3s; vote emitted at :1026-1030. It NEVER checks `resp.Truncated` and NEVER retries over TCP (:978-1031) — and the DNS library it uses documents exactly that: "Exchange does not retry a failed query, nor will it fall back to TCP in case of truncation" (miekg/dns client.go:42-43). Cloudflare does not honour the advertised 4096: its OPT echoes `udp: 1232`, so a bigger buffer cannot fix this — only TCP (or DoH) can. THE DECISIVE CONTROL PAIR, same host, same resolver, same query, only TC-handling differs: analyzer shape (`+ignore +notcp`) → `qr tc rd ra`, ANSWER:0, 51 bytes, NO AD; dig shape (TCP retry on TC=1) → `qr rd ra ad`, ANSWER:2, 2572 bytes, AD PRESENT. Cloudflare clears AD on the record-stripped truncation stub, and the analyzer votes that stub as an affirmative `ad_absent` — a claim of absence produced by an answer it never received. ALL THREE OBSERVATIONS EXPLAINED: nlnetlabs.nl (alg 8) is 229/263 bytes, never truncates, `ad` present at all four reachable resolvers → `secure` (the defect is SIZE-gated, not parse-gated, which is why the analyzer looks correct on every ordinary zone); the pq-dualds 3/2 split is per-resolver AD-on-truncated-stub policy, not validation — Cloudflare clears AD on the stub → ad_absent, Quad9 and DNS4EU KEEP AD on the stub → secure, OpenDNS returns the full 2903-byte answer → secure, exactly the Day-5/6/7 pattern. IT IS AN OMISSION, NOT A DESIGN CHOICE: two sibling query paths in the same file already carry the RFC 7766 TC→TCP retry with comments naming "large signed RRsets (DNSKEY/TLSA)" (client.go:1531-1541 and :730-738); the AD probe is the one path that was missed. THE FIX (dns-tool-intel's lane, not mine to write): add the TC=1 → TCP retry to `adVoteFor`; POSITIVE CONTROL — with the retry, Cloudflare reads `ad` on both legs for pq, pq2 AND pq-dualds, so the vote becomes `secure`; NEGATIVE CONTROL — microsoft.com and amazon.com (unsigned) still return no `ad` with the retry in place and MUST stay `ad_absent`, and dnssec-failed.org still SERVFAILs, preserving the bogus branch. SECOND, INDEPENDENT DEFECT found in the same function: the SOA fallback at :1016-1030 overwrites `resp` on `err2 == nil` WITHOUT re-checking Rcode, so a SERVFAIL on the SOA re-query lands at the AD read and is voted `ad_absent`, bypassing the bogus/unmeasured branch at :1001-1008 entirely. ALSO: DoH endpoints are configured for Cloudflare/Google/DNS4EU but never used for the AD vote — DoH has no 1232 ceiling, so routing the probe there fixes it too. WHY THIS MATTERS BEYOND OUR FIXTURES: any signed zone whose answer exceeds ~1232 bytes at a resolver that clears AD on truncation is misreported as unauthenticated. That is not a PQ-only bug; it is every large signed RRset, for every user of the tool. The instrument HAS an `unmeasured` state (Quad9/pq uses it) and should have used it. Our own conclusions are unaffected: "Cloudflare validates alg-18" came from dig, not the tool. CONFIDENCE: code read first-hand at file:line, wire controls run first-hand from this machine; adversarial verification (two skeptics) is re-running now after the network blackout killed the first attempt — I will record their verdict, including a refutation. | workflow wf_db1a23cb map:query-bits (evidence array, all verified=true); wire runs 2026-09-03 | @(this commit)

2026-09-03T17:28Z | claude-code | VERIFICATION OF THE ad_absent DIAGNOSIS — DONE BY ME AT SOURCE, because both workflow attempts to run skeptics died on infrastructure (first a local DNS blackout, then a structured-output retry cap). I read the code myself via the GitHub API instead, which for a claim of this kind is stronger than a second opinion. CONFIRMED, first-hand: `adVoteFor` contains ZERO occurrences of `Truncated`; its only exchange is `dnsClient.Exchange(ctx, msg, protoUDP, addr)` — UDP, no retry; and two sibling paths in the same file DO check `r.Truncated` and fall back to TCP (client.go:731-734 and :1533-1536, both returning the file's own `errTruncatedTCP` constant defined at :64-68). Omission, not design, confirmed. The vote tail is verbatim: `if resp.AuthenticatedData { votes <- "secure" } else { votes <- "ad_absent" }`. ||| AMENDMENT AGAINST MY OWN 17:2xZ ENTRY [WRONG FILM, kept and labeled]: I named the cause "truncation with no TCP retry". That is incomplete. The reproduction (41 dig queries from this machine, 17:12–17:15Z) shows the mechanism needs a PAIR, and neither half alone does it: `+dnssec` alone → AD=1 (dig's own TCP retry recovers the bit, 5091 bytes); `+ignore` alone → AD=1 (without DO=1 the answer is 98 bytes and never truncates); `+bufsize=512` alone → AD=1; `+noedns` alone → AD=1; ONLY `+dnssec +ignore` together → `qr tc rd ra`, AD=0, 51 bytes. So: DO=1 is what INFLATES the answer past Cloudflare's 1232-byte ceiling (pq qtype A with the NSEC3 denial is 5091 bytes; SOA is 2572), and the missing TCP retry is what makes the tool CONSUME the resulting stub. The analyzer sets DO=1 deliberately and correctly — it must, to see RRSIGs — which is why the defect only appears in the one code path that asks for DNSSEC data without being able to receive it. The control row survives the pair: nlnetlabs.nl `+dnssec +ignore` → AD=1, 229 bytes, still correct. ||| SECOND DEFECT CONFIRMED BY MY OWN READ, and it is worse than I wrote: the `resp.Rcode != dns.RcodeSuccess → unmeasured` guard sits BEFORE the fallback, so it protects only the first response. The fallback then does `if err2 == nil { resp = r2 }` and falls straight through to the AD read. A SERVFAIL on the SOA re-query is therefore voted `ad_absent` — bypassing the function's own careful SERVFAIL handling above it, which re-queries with CD=1 to separate a genuine validation rejection (`bogus`) from transport failure (`unmeasured`). The author thought hard about ambiguity in one branch and the other branch walks around it. ||| TWO LIVE OBSERVATIONS FROM THE CONTROL RUNS, both bearing on other lanes' open items. (1) QUAD9 FLAPPED INSIDE TWO MINUTES: `dig @9.9.9.9 pq.resolutionscope.com A` read AD=1 at 17:1xZ and AD=0 about two minutes later, same box, same query, no flags changed; pq2 read AD=0 both times. That is an independent third observation of the transience SciSpace reported and hermes ruled TRANSIENT/NOT DEPLOYED — and it sharpens the ruling: the instability is at the TWO-MINUTE scale, so a daily-cadence single read of Quad9 can land on either face. B6/B8's hold looks right; any future Quad9 claim needs repeated reads within one session, not one read per day. (2) GOOGLE WAS UNREACHABLE from this machine for the whole window: every `@8.8.8.8` query timed out, including google.com itself. Stated as a vantage limitation, not a finding about Google — and a reminder that the repro's Google column is unmeasured, not negative. | client.go fetched and read at source; 41 dig runs 17:12–17:15Z; workflow journal repro record | @(this commit)

WHERE I DIFFER: Science argues corpus's Dot/Doh/Doq/Doh3 should be canonical because they are the field's standard names. That property is the re-validation doctrine implemented, and it is pinned in tests (resolver_choice.rs:121 asserts identity() == "9.9.9.9/tls/dns.quad9.net"). Renaming the seal suffix to doh/dot either breaks the round-trip law or forces a SECOND spelling inside one flag, which is worse for the four archetypes than two vocabularies on two surfaces. Scale of the change, measured: 108 Transport references in engine/, 42 in cli/, 0 anywhere else. RECOMMENDATION: keep both vocabularies, one per audience, and make the seam TOTAL and compiler-enforced — export Transport from types (hermes, one line), then `impl From<engine::Transport> for types::corpus::Transport` with an exhaustive match and no wildcard plus a test over both ALL arrays, so a seventh transport breaks the build instead of silently entering the corpus unmapped. Science's audience argument survives exactly where it applies: the published corpus row says doh. THE BLOCKER, verified by me at source on origin/main: `zone_contains_host` gates on `z.contains('.')` as a proxy for "at least two labels, so not a bare TLD". `co.uk` contains a dot, so a genuinely non-existent `nonexistent.co.uk` — whose NXDOMAIN is answered by the co.uk REGISTRY servers — passes the guard and grades "record absent, zone exists" for a domain that does not exist. My fix would have traded one false claim for its mirror image. IT IS NOT FIXABLE BY TIGHTENING THE HEURISTIC, and that is the part that matters: `support.google.com` with SOA `google.com` and `nonexistent.co.uk` with SOA `co.uk` are STRUCTURALLY IDENTICAL — both "scanned name is a direct child of the SOA zone" — with opposite correct answers. No label count or suffix rule separates a registry suffix from an ordinary parent zone without the Public Suffix List. PRE-EXISTING, NOT INTRODUCED: `zone_contains_host` is on main today serving the DANE attribution path, so the false positive already ships; my PR would have WIDENED it to TLS-RPT and, via `record_absence_verdict`, to SPF, DMARC, MTA-STS, CAA and — a confirmed finding I had not enumerated — DKIM. NEW ITEM for the board, severity above my own PR: main misgrades non-existent domains under every multi-label registry suffix in the DANE path today. NEXT ROUND, only the sound half: (1) NXDOMAIN grades RecordAbsent ONLY when the SOA zone EQUALS the scanned domain — sound with no dependency, since the zone answered for itself; (2) the proper-ancestor case is undecidable from the packet and gets an honest Indet, not a guess, until it gets a MEASUREMENT (one lookup of the scanned domain's own SOA), which is a separate change; (3) the DANE exposure gets its own item. That keeps the headline fix — apple.com and the rest stop being told they do not exist — and drops the sub-label improvement, which was never soundly inferable. Also carried: DKIM unlisted in the body and the semantics entry; `tlsa_err_to_count`'s doc comment still claims exact equality, which the PR made false; the SOA read open-coded at three sites with no choke point; the semantics entry asserting NoZone stays reachable for a genuinely absent domain, which the blocker makes false. AND ONE HONEST CORRECTION the implementer surfaced against my own brief: I wrote that a sub-label scan turns SPF, DMARC, MTA-STS and CAA all TransientError at once; re-measured against 1.1.1.1, for support.google.com only the underscore names NXDOMAIN, while TXT and CAA answer NOERROR because the name itself exists — so only DMARC and MTA-STS move there. 2026-09-04T05:21Z | claude-code | RELEASE-METADATA ITEM CLOSED, VERIFIED FROM THE OUTSIDE. Carey edited both releases; I checked what a stranger actually receives rather than trusting the flags. Both now read `prerelease=true, latest=false`, and `GET /repos/…/releases/latest` returns **404 Not Found** — which is the CORRECT and intended state: GitHub's "latest" endpoint excludes prereleases, so with no stable release published there is nothing to advertise. A 404 is the honest answer to "what is your current stable release?" when there isn't one; `prerelease: false` on an alpha was a false answer to the same question. An installer that keyed on that endpoint now fails loudly instead of silently fetching an alpha one hundred commits stale — the fail-closed direction, arrived at from the metadata side. WITH #46 ON MAIN, BOTH HALVES NOW EXIST: the instances are fixed (Carey's edits) and the producer can no longer recreate the defect (the tag-derived class rule, fail-closed on an unrecognised shape, plus the post-publish read-back). That is the pattern I keep having to relearn today — fix the instance AND the producer, or the producer wins. ONE MAINTAINER ITEM LEFT ON CAREY'S BOARD: main is still unprotected (`branches/main/protection` → 404 "Branch not protected"), so `ci-ok` — built to be the one context branch protection can require — is required by nothing, and a red build can land. The trade-off I flagged stands and is real: classic required checks reject direct pushes, which would route every ledger append through a PR and change how this mesh writes. That is a genuine cost, not a formality, and it is why this one is a judgement rather than an obvious yes. | gh release list; GET /releases/latest; branches/main/protection | @(this commit)
